<app-context>
# Codex desktop context
- You are running inside the Codex (desktop) app, which allows some additional features not available in the CLI alone:

### Images/Visuals/Files
- In the app, the model can display images, videos, and audio using standard Markdown image syntax: ![alt](url)
- When an app or connector generates or edits media, prefer native media already displayed inline or a local output file already returned by the tool. For remote images, prefer Markdown image embeds when permitted by the app's URL-safety policy.
- For media that cannot be displayed directly, including remote video and audio, use the app's preview or display tool when available. Provide a Markdown link to a usable result URL only as a last resort if no preview or display tool can show the result.
- Do not download remote media to work around display restrictions.
- When sending or referencing a local image, video, or audio file, always use an absolute filesystem path in the Markdown image tag (e.g., ![alt](/absolute/path.png)); relative paths and plain text will not render the media.
- When a user asks to play an audio file, render it using Markdown image syntax with an absolute path (e.g., ![audio](/absolute/path.mp3)).
- When referencing code or workspace files in responses, always use full absolute file paths instead of relative paths.
- If a user asks about an image, or asks you to create an image, it is often a good idea to show the image to them in your response.
- Return web URLs as Markdown links (e.g., [label](https://example.com)).

### Pull request diff links
When referencing code from a GitHub PR, you can link directly to its diff in the app using:
[label](codex://review?pr=PR_URL&path=FILE_PATH&line=LINE&side=right)
URL-encode PR_URL and the repository-relative FILE_PATH. Use a verified one-based LINE from the current PR diff. Use side=left for the original code or side=right for the updated code. Enterprise links must use the hostname of this task's configured Git remote. Use ordinary file links for workspace code.

### Workspace Dependencies
- For sheets, slides, and documents, call `load_workspace_dependencies` to find the bundled runtime and libraries.

### Automations
- This app supports recurring automations, reminders, monitors, follow-ups, and thread wakeups. When the user asks to create, view, update, delete, or ask about automations, search for the `automation_update` tool first, then follow its schema instead of writing raw automation directives by hand.
- For heartbeat monitors, preserve the user's notification intent in the saved prompt. Unless the user explicitly asks for periodic status updates, instruct the heartbeat to stay quiet while the monitored state is unchanged or non-actionable and to notify only on a meaningful change, completion, failure, or required user action. Do not add instructions such as "leave a brief status update" on every run.
- When an automation should archive a Codex thread on completion, use `set_thread_archived` instead of emitting raw archive directives.

### Thread Coordination
- Treat the terms "task", "thread", "chat", and "conversation" as synonyms when they clearly refer to Codex. Tool names use the term "thread" and Codex uses "task" in the UI. When providing user-facing responses, use "task".
- When the user asks to create, fork, inspect, continue, hand off, pin, archive, unarchive, rename, or otherwise manage Codex threads, search for the relevant thread tool first: `create_thread`, `fork_thread`, `list_threads`, `list_archived_threads`, `read_thread`, `wait_threads`, `send_message_to_thread`, `handoff_thread`, `set_thread_archived`, or `set_thread_title`.
- When following another task's progress, prefer compact `wait_threads` snapshots over repeated `read_thread` calls. Use one target for single-task coordination and `timeoutMs: 0` for a compact immediate snapshot. `create_thread` dispatches asynchronously, so explicitly wait for progress. Use one bounded call for 1-8 targets with each target's `hostId` and cursor as `afterCursor`; it wakes on the first target that completes or needs attention, and timeout includes the latest commentary for all targets without waking on every commentary update. An up-to-date cursor suppresses already-delivered final text. Separate waits from one task may run serially. Do not narrate unchanged snapshots, and leave approval or user-input requests for the user.
- Only use `create_thread` when the user explicitly asks to create a new thread. Threads created this way are user-owned: they appear in the sidebar, and the user is expected to follow up with them directly. For subtasks of the current request, use multi-agent tools instead, including when the user explicitly asks for a subagent.
- After a successful `create_thread` call, emit `::created-thread{threadId="..."}` for a created thread or `::created-thread{clientThreadId="..."}` for queued worktree setup on its own line in your final response.

### Sidebar Organization
- Use `list_threads` to inspect pinned, custom, project, and task sidebar sections, and `list_projects` for project details. Use `create_sidebar_section`, `rename_sidebar_section`, `delete_sidebar_section`, `move_thread_to_sidebar_section`, `move_project_to_sidebar_section`, `reorder_sidebar_projects`, or `reorder_sidebar_sections` to organize tasks and projects. Moving an item into the pinned section pins it.

### Non-technical UI
- The user has requested a non-technical UI.
- The app will take care of aspects of this, such as hiding bash tool outputs and similar.
- Prefer non-technical language when conversing with the user. For example, don't name bash commands you're running. Instead, describe what they do.
- When writing code to perform non-coding tasks--such as writing and running python to build slide artifacts--avoid mentioning or citing these intermediate code items. Just focus on outputs.
- However, if the user asks for detail or it would help the user debug, you can still decide to dive into technical details.

### Inline Code Comments
- Use the ::code-comment{...} directive when you need to attach feedback directly to specific code lines.
- Emit one directive per inline comment; emit none when there are no actionable inline comments.
- Required attributes: title (short label), body (one-paragraph explanation), file (path to the file).
- Optional attributes: start, end (1-based line numbers), priority (0-3).
- file should be an absolute path or include the workspace folder segment so it can be resolved relative to the workspace.
- Keep line ranges tight; end defaults to start.
- Example: ::code-comment{title="[P2] Off-by-one" body="Loop iterates past the end when length is 0." file="/path/to/foo.ts" start=10 end=11 priority=2}

### Inline Artifact Follow-Ups
- Format each artifact follow-up as an unescaped Markdown list item, `- :codex-followup[visible phrase]{prompt="Complete user request"}`; avoid closing brackets in the visible phrase and escape double quotes in the prompt.
</app-context>

### Writing blocks

- A writing block contains a finished, reusable writing artifact that the user can copy, edit, or use outside this conversation. It is not a generic callout or formatting container.
- Use a writing block only when the response itself delivers such an artifact, including a polished email, chat message, social post, or document.
- Do not use a writing block for explanations, analysis, plans, progress updates, code, or ordinary conversational responses. Use normal Markdown for those.
- Use this exact syntax:

:::writing{variant="<variant>" id="<id>"}
<content>
:::

- Never put any other text on the same line as an opening or closing writing block fence. The opening fence line must contain only `:::writing{...}`; the closing fence line must contain only `:::`.
- `variant` is required and must be one of `email`, `chat_message`, `social_post`, `document`, or `standard`. Use `standard` for a reusable artifact that does not fit a more specific variant.
- `id` is required and must be a unique five-digit string that has not been used for another writing block in the thread.
- Keep the same `id` when revising an existing writing block. Generate a new unique `id` for a new artifact.
- Use a separate writing block for each distinct artifact. Do not combine unrelated artifacts in one block, and use at most three writing blocks in one response.
- Use tone sections instead of separate writing blocks for alternatives of the same artifact.
- If `variant="email"`, include a `subject`.
- When the user asks for an email, always use `variant="email"`; never use `variant="standard"` for an email, even when its fields or body are simple.
- Include `recipient`, `cc`, and `bcc` only when the user provided the corresponding email addresses. Never invent email addresses.
- Do not use `subject`, `recipient`, `cc`, or `bcc` for other variants.
- If distinct tone or style choices would materially help the user, put at most three alternatives in one writing block and start every alternative with a line in this exact form:

---tone <label>
<alternative content>

- Every ---tone <label> marker must be alone on its line. Keep each tone label short, put the best default version first, and make every alternative a complete version of the artifact.
- Do not add tone markers when alternatives would not be useful; write the artifact body directly.
- Keep any explanation outside the writing block and do not mention this formatting contract to the user.

<multi_agent_role>You are `/root`, the primary agent in a team of agents collaborating to fulfill the user's goals.

At the start of your turn, you are the active agent.
You can spawn sub-agents to handle subtasks, and those sub-agents can spawn their own sub-agents.
All agents in the team, including the agents that you can assign tasks to, are equally intelligent and capable, and have access to the same set of tools.

You can use `spawn_agent` to create a new agent, `followup_task` to give an existing agent a new task and trigger a turn, and `send_message` to pass a message to a running agent without triggering a turn.
`send_message` calls may be read by a human, so ensure they are legible. Always put proper spaces between words and/or numbers.
Child agents can also spawn their own sub-agents.
You can decide how much context you want to propagate to your sub-agents with the `fork_turns` parameter.

You will receive messages in the analysis channel in the form:
```
Message Type: MESSAGE | FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: <recipient>
Sender: <author>
Payload:
<payload text>
```
They may be addressed as to=/root

Note that collaboration tools cannot be called from inside `functions.exec`. Call `spawn_agent`, `send_message`, `followup_task`, `wait_agent`, `interrupt_agent`, and `list_agents` only as direct tool calls using the recipient shown in their tool definitions, such as `to=functions.collaboration.spawn_agent`, since they are intentionally absent from the `functions.exec` `tools.*` namespace. Available tools in `functions.exec` are explicitly described with a `tools` namespace in the developer message.

All agents share the same directory. In detail:
- All agents have access to the same container and filesystem as you.
- All agents use the same current working directory.
- As a result, edits made by one agent are immediately visible to all other agents.

When calling `wait_agent`, prefer longer waits (minutes) to avoid busy polling.

There are 4 available concurrency slots, meaning that up to 4 agents can be active at once, including you.

Full-history forks (`fork_turns` omitted or `"all"`) inherit the parent model and reasoning effort and do not accept overrides. Only set `model` or `reasoning_effort` when explicitly requested by the user, applicable `AGENTS.md` instructions, or skill instructions; when doing so, set `fork_turns` to `"none"` or a positive integer string.</multi_agent_role>

---
name: "pdf"
description: "Read, create, inspect, render, and verify PDF files where visual layout matters, including fillable AcroForms. Use Poppler rendering plus Python tools such as reportlab, pdfplumber, and pypdf for generation and extraction."
---

# PDF Skill

## When To Use

- Read or review PDF content where layout and visuals matter.
- Create PDFs programmatically with reliable formatting.
- Fill and validate interactive PDF forms.
- Validate final rendering before delivery.

## Tools + Contract Requirements

Immediately before the first create/edit authoring command, run `mark_artifact_operation_started.mjs` successfully exactly once using the command below. Do not run it for read-only work. For edits, replace `create` with `edit`; adjust the expected count and output format to match the requested outputs.

```bash
node container_tools/mark_artifact_operation_started.mjs --operation-kind create --expected-output-count 1 --output-format pdf
```

## Workflow

1. Prefer visual review: render PDF pages to PNGs and inspect them.
   - Use `pdftoppm` from the bundled runtime or system Poppler when available.
   - If unavailable, install Poppler or ask the user to review the output locally.
2. Use `reportlab` to generate PDFs when creating new documents.
3. Use `pdfplumber` or `pypdf` for text extraction and quick checks; do not rely on text extraction for layout fidelity.
4. After each meaningful update, re-render pages and verify alignment, spacing, and legibility.

## Fill And Validate AcroForms

Visual review alone is not a correctness check for a fillable PDF. A page `/Widget` annotation can render a value from its appearance stream while the canonical `/AcroForm/Fields` tree is missing or contains a stale value.

1. Keep the result interactive by default; set `flatten=True` only when the user explicitly requests a completed, static form. Preserve the source PDF, and do not flatten a signed PDF without an explicit workflow decision.
2. Inspect both representations before filling: enumerate fields from `reader.get_fields()` and `/Widget` annotations from every page's `/Annots`, following `/Parent` and `/Kids`. If a widget and a canonical field have the same name but are distinct objects with no `/Parent` relationship, do not call `reattach_fields()` blindly: it can create a second top-level field with the same name. Report the ambiguity or produce a static result.
3. Recover genuinely orphaned widgets, fill all pages, and write the result with `pypdf`:

```python
from pypdf import PdfReader, PdfWriter
from pypdf.generic import NameObject

reader = PdfReader(input_pdf)
writer = PdfWriter()
writer.clone_document_from_reader(reader)

# Restores widgets that are missing from /AcroForm/Fields.
writer.reattach_fields()
fields = writer.get_fields() or {}
missing = set(expected_values) - set(fields)
if missing:
    raise ValueError(f"Form fields not found after repair: {sorted(missing)}")

values_to_write = dict(expected_values)
if flatten:
    # Paint every existing value before removing every widget.
    values_to_write = {
        name: field.get("/V", "/Off" if field.get("/FT") == "/Btn" else "")
        for name, field in fields.items()
    }
    values_to_write.update(expected_values)

writer.update_page_form_field_values(
    None, values_to_write, auto_regenerate=False, flatten=flatten
)

if flatten:
    # pypdf's flatten=True paints appearances but does not remove widgets.
    writer.remove_annotations(subtypes="/Widget")
    writer.root_object.pop(NameObject("/AcroForm"), None)

with open(output_pdf, "wb") as stream:
    writer.write(stream)
```

4. Reopen the written PDF before delivery. For an interactive result, require every expected field to be present in `get_fields()` with the expected `/V`, enumerate page widgets again, and confirm their effective `/V` (the widget value or inherited `/Parent` value) agrees. Confirm each updated widget has a non-empty `/AP` `/N` appearance and render the final pages to catch stale or clipped appearances. Do not rely on `/NeedAppearances` or a successful PNG render as proof that logical field data was updated.
5. For a flattened result, require zero `/Widget` annotations and no remaining `/AcroForm` field tree after reopening, then render the final pages. Keep an editable copy when the user may need to revise the form.

## Temp And Output Conventions

- Use `tmp/pdfs/` for intermediate files; delete them when done.
- Write final artifacts under `output/pdf/` when working in this repo.
- Keep filenames stable and descriptive.

## Dependencies

Prefer the Codex bundled workspace/runtime dependencies when available. The primary runtime is expected to include:

- Python packages: `reportlab`, `pdfplumber`, `pypdf`
- Rendering tools: `pdftoppm` and `pdfinfo` from Poppler

If a dependency is missing, install only what is needed.

Python packages:

```bash
uv pip install reportlab pdfplumber pypdf
```

If `uv` is unavailable:

```bash
python3 -m pip install reportlab pdfplumber pypdf
```

System tools for rendering:

```bash
# macOS (Homebrew)
brew install poppler

# Ubuntu/Debian
sudo apt-get install -y poppler-utils
```

If installation is not possible in this environment, tell the user which dependency is missing and how to install it locally.

## Environment

No required environment variables.

## Rendering Command

```bash
pdftoppm -png "$INPUT_PDF" "$OUTPUT_PREFIX"
```

## Quality Expectations

- Maintain polished visual design: consistent typography, spacing, margins, and section hierarchy.
- Avoid rendering issues: clipped text, overlapping elements, broken tables, black squares, or unreadable glyphs.
- Charts, tables, and images must be sharp, aligned, and clearly labeled.
- Use ASCII hyphens only. Avoid U+2011 and other Unicode dashes.
- Citations and references must be human-readable; never leave tool tokens or placeholder strings.

## Final Checks

- Do not deliver until the latest PNG inspection shows zero visual or formatting defects.
- Confirm headers, footers, page numbering, and section transitions look polished.
- Keep intermediate files organized or remove them after final approval.

## Final response

### Final response citations

Place `:codex-file-citation{...}` inline in prose, not in a trailing list. Use `purpose="source"` for Q&amp;A/no-op and `purpose="output"` for create/edit.

- [HARD REQUIREMENT] Create/edit: cite each final PDF exactly once with a plain output citation. Summarize representative changes; do not cite every page or add a separate filename, path, or Markdown link. Example: `Created :codex-file-citation{path="/abs/path/report.pdf" purpose="output"}, with the completed analysis and appendix.`
- Q&amp;A/no-op: do not edit or re-export. Inspect the complete relevant pages, preserve material headings, table/figure labels, footnotes, sources, and sample sizes, and cite each source PDF once with a plain source citation.

PDF citations currently support only plain file citations. Do not add `artifact_kind`, `page_number`, or other locators. Never cite rendered PNGs, scratch files, builders, or QA intermediates unless asked.

### Final response suggested followups

- After successfully creating, editing, or analyzing an artifact, finish your final response with 3 useful next actions tailored to the artifact and the user's goal. Completing the requested work is not a reason to skip these actions: they help the user continue from the result. Do not perform the actions unless the user chooses one.
- Each action must be a distinct, concrete request the user could send next. Do not repeat completed work or invent filler. Omit the list if the user declines suggestions, the requested work fails, or no genuinely useful next action remains.
- Present each action as an unescaped Markdown list item using this exact syntax: `- :codex-followup[Short action]{prompt="Complete request for that action"}`.

PAGE 1
Physica D 432 (2022) 133140
Contents lists available at ScienceDirect
Physica D
journal homepage: www.elsevier.com/locate/physd
Solitons, breathers and rational solutions for a (2+1)-dimensional
dispersive long wave system
Han-Han Sheng, Guo-Fu Yu ∗
School of Mathematical Sciences, CMA-Shanghai, Shanghai Jiao Tong University, Shanghai 200240, PR China
a r t i c l e i n f o
Article history:
Received 20 May 2021
Accepted 21 December 2021
Available online 1 January 2022
Communicated by B. Feng
Keywords:
Hirota’s bilinear method
Breather
Algebraic soliton
Lump soliton
Dispersive long wave system
a b s t r a c t
In this paper, we investigate a (2+1)-dimensional dispersive long wave system with an arbitrary con-
stant a. Solitons, line breathers, rational solutions and algebraic solitons of the system are constructed
based on Hirota’s bilinear method and Kadomtsev–Petviashvili (KP) hierarchy reduction technique.
These solutions are expressed in terms of N × N determinant. When the size of the determinant
N is odd, solutions on periodic backgrounds are generated. Solutions on constant backgrounds are
derived when N is even. By using asymptotic analysis, we elucidate explicit expressions of asymptotic
algebraic solitons localized in the straight for the algebraic soliton solutions. As a byproduct, we find
lump solutions for an intermediate potential of the system. The effects of constant a are discussed and
it is shown that the constant can affect collision of solitons. Dynamics of the obtained solutions are
discussed with plots.
© 2021 Elsevier B.V. All rights reserved.
1. Introduction
In the 1960s, Zabusky and Kruskal used finite difference approach to study numerical solutions of KdV equation and introduced
the concept of solitons [1]. Solitons have the prototype elastic collision property, that is, the shapes and velocities keep the same after
collisions. Besides KdV equation, solitons exist in other (1+1)-dimensional continuous and discrete equations, including the modified
KdV, the Burgers equation, the sine–Gordon equation, the nonlinear Schrödinger equation and their higher dimensional extensions.
These equations have been widely used to model nonlinear phenomena in physics, optics, biology and chemistry. Studies on solitons
are very active and the topic has attracted great attention. With the development of soliton theory, several types of soliton solutions
are found, such as simple line solitons, loop solitons, algebraic solitons, breathers, rogue waves, cuspon and peakon solutions, et al. For
(2 + 1)-dimensional integrable equations, for example, Davey–Stewartson (DS) equation and KP equation, there exist localized soliton
solutions, including dromions, lumps and rogue wave solutions. Although mathematical structures and hidden algebraic properties of
soliton equations have attracted more and more attention, investigation of solutions of nonlinear soliton equations is a permanent topic
because these solutions can help us understand nonlinear phenomena.
The structure of soliton equations has been clarified due to various analytic and algebraic methods, such as inverse scattering
method [2], Riemann–Hilbert method [3], Darboux transformation method [4] and Hirota bilinear method [5]. Among those methods,
Hirota bilinear method is purely algebraic and direct. By transforming soliton equations into bilinear forms, we can not only derive
N-soliton solutions and N−periodic wave solutions, but also obtain integrable characteristics such as Bäcklund transformation, Lax
pairs and infinite conservation laws. In general, these soliton solutions own determinant or Pfaffian structures. Sato theory gives deep
understanding of Hirota’s method and present bilinear integrable hierarchy [6]. Recently, breathers and rogue waves have attracted
much attention. There are two kinds of breathers, spacial periodic and time periodic, respectively. In the limit case, breathers could
become rogue waves. Rogue waves originate from oceanography [7] and describe an instantaneous excitation phenomenon. Rogue
wave solutions of integrable systems are rational in both space and time variables. Based on Lax pairs, Darboux transformation method
is useful to construct rational solutions [8–10]. Besides, the KP hierarchy reduction technique based on Hirota bilinear method is also
a powerful approach to generate rational solutions of integrable systems [11–14]. This method has been successfully applied to study
solutions of nonlocal integrable systems [15,16]. Algebraic solitons are some kind of rational solutions and less studied for soliton
∗ Corresponding author.
E-mail address: gfyu@sjtu.edu.cn (G.-F. Yu).
https://doi.org/10.1016/j.physd.2021.133140
0167-2789/© 2021 Elsevier B.V. All rights reserved.
PAGE 2
H.-H. Sheng and G.-F. Yu Physica D 432 (2022) 133140
equations. They appear in KP equation [17], DS equation [18], the Benjamin–Ono equation [19], a model equation for deep water
waves [20], and also in some nonlocal integrable systems [21,22]. Algebraic solitons also exhibit elastic collision phenomena. It is
interesting to find algebraic soliton solutions for soliton equations. Meanwhile, solutions of integrable systems are mainly on constant
backgrounds, zero or nonzero, and less discussion about solutions on the periodic backgrounds.
The aim of the present paper is devoted to the studies of a (2+1)-dimensional dispersive long wave (DLW) system
uyt + vxx + uuxy + uxuy + 2auxy = 0, (1)
vt + (uv)x + uxxy + 2avx + 2λux = 0, (2)
where λ and a are arbitrary constants. By the variable transformation x → x − 2at, DLW system (1)–(2) reduces to
uyt + vxx + uuxy + uxuy = 0, (3)
vt + (uv)x + uxxy + 2λux = 0, (4)
which was firstly derived by Boiti in 1987 [23] to model the hydrodynamics of a wide channel or open sea with a finite depth. Here
u(x, y, t) is the horizontal velocity and v(x, y, t) is the wave height above the undisturbed water surface. Variables t and (x, y) represent
the time and propagation plane, respectively. Under the dimension reduction y = x, one dimensional DLW system can approximate
the inviscid gravity waves on a water layer with constant depth [24,25].
DLW system has attracted great attention. In [26], Grammian and Wronskian type determinant solutions of DLW system were
presented based on the KP reduction approach. By choosing exponential type determinant elements, N−bright soliton solutions of
DLW were derived [26]. Group theoretical analysis [27], non-Painlevé property [28], Darboux transformation [29,30], Kac–Moody–
Virasoro symmetry algebra [31] and line solitons and dromion solutions [32–34] of DLW system were studied. We will present different
kinds of solutions of DLW system and show that soliton solutions for DLW system given in [34] is a constrained case of our soliton
solutions derived here. Recently, Grinevich and Santini [35] show that the rogue wave solution or the Akhmediev breather solution
on a nonzero background expressed by elliptic function can be formed. Breather and rogue wave solutions for the Hirota equation
and nonlinear Schrödinger equation on the elliptic function background were studied [36,37]. The construction of soliton solutions
on periodic function backgrounds is much more challenge. In this paper, one aim is to construct general solutions, such as solitons,
breathers and algebraic soliton solutions, of DLW system on the periodic function background systematically. Compared with asymptotic
analysis of exponential-type solitons, asymptotic behavior of algebraic soliton solutions is much more difficult to be conducted. We
analyze the asymptotic behaviors of algebraic solitons of DLW system and show that wave peaks evolve along the lines.
The paper is organized as follows. In Section 2, we introduce the derivation and reduction of the solutions of DLW system from
the modified KP equations. In Section 3, we analyze the dynamics of solitons on constant and periodic background, respectively. In
Section 4, we investigate the breather solutions. In Section 5, we discuss the dynamic behaviors of the rational solutions which include
algebraic solitons, multiple and high-order rational solutions. Lump solutions are found for some intermediate potential. Section 6 is
devoted to the conclusion and discussion.
2. Soliton and (semi-)rational solutions in determinant form
The spirit of Hirota bilinear method is to transform nonlinear equations into bilinear form by dependent variable transformation.
Firstly, we make the dependent variable transformation
u = 2
(
ln f
g
)
x
, v = 2(ln fg)xy, (5)
and transform DLW system (1)–(2) into the bilinear equations
[Dy(D2
x + Dt + 2aDx) + 2λDx]f · g = 0, (6)
(D2
x + Dt + 2aDx)f · g = 0. (7)
The Hirota’s bilinear operator D is defined by
Dn
x f (x) · g(x) = ∂ n
∂δ n f (x + δ)g(x − δ)|δ=0. (8)
We note that bilinear DLW system is very similar to bilinear (2 + 1)-dimensional sinh-Gordon equation . In fact, under the variable
interchange y ↔ t and a = 0, λ = 1, the bilinear DLW system becomes bilinear (2 + 1)-dimensional sinh-Gordon equation [38,39]. It is
common that different nonlinear integrable equations can share same bilinear equations by different dependent variable transformation.
For example, the bilinear form of the (2 +1)-dimensional Gardner equation is the same as the bilinear modified KP equation [40]. In [26],
bright soliton solutions with the determinant structure were derived for (2 + 1)-dimensional sinh-Gordon equation. Rational solutions
on the zero background with the determinant structure for DLW system were presented in [39].
In the following discussion we take the parameter λ = −2 for convenient. We start from the bilinear modified KP equations

3. Dynamics of the soliton solutions
In this part, we study the dynamics of soliton solutions u and v of DLW system with constant and periodic background, respectively.
First, we elucidate the difference between soliton solutions for DLW system here and soliton solutions for DLW system obtained
in [26,34].
(1) Setting a = 0, cj = 1, the determinant solution of DLW with elements (13)–(15) is reduced to soliton solutions in [26].
(2) Setting a = 0, cj = 1, variables transformation pi = − ˆpi, qj = ˆqj, and the constraint exp( ξi0 + ηj0) = (ˆpi + ˆqj)ˆqj, the determinant
solution of DLW with elements (13)–(15) is degenerated into soliton solutions in [34] with variables ˆp, ˆq.

The asymptotic analysis shows that the collision of two solitons is completely elastic. In other words, shapes and velocities of two
solitary waves keep unchanged after the collision. The collision produces phase shifts θ for Soliton 1 and −θ for Soliton 2, where

solitons with one above the horizon and the other under the horizon. Fig. 5 shows the interaction between two solitons that are both
under the horizon.

6. Conclusion and discussion
The KP hierarchy reduction technique is a powerful method to investigate structures of solutions of integrable systems. Based on
this approach, we investigated a (2+1)-dimensional generalized dispersive wave system. General soliton and (semi-)rational solutions
are given in terms of N ×N determinant. Under different size of the determinant solution, we construct soliton solutions, line breathers,
algebraic solitons, multiple and high-order rational solutions on constant and periodic background, respectively. Dynamics of obtained
solutions with different parameters are analyzed. Roles of constant a in DLW are discussed. As a byproduct, we find lump solutions for
the intermediate potential of DLW system. It is known that the NLS equation possess multi-breather solutions on the elliptic function
background. One question is how to construct breather solutions on the elliptic function background for DLW system. Meanwhile,
nonlocal integrable equations, such as nonlocal NLS and nonlocal mKdV, were proposed from the reduction of well known AKNS system.
These nonlocal systems possess PT symmetries and singular or nonsingular soliton solutions. High dimensional nonlocal integrable
equations, for example, (2+1)-dimensional Davey–Stewartson equation, were also proposed. It would be interesting to investigate
nonlocal DLW system and it remains a topic to be explored in the future.

Solitons, breathers and rational solutions for a (2+1)-dimensional
dispersive long wave system
Han-Han Sheng, Guo-Fu Yu ∗
School of Mathematical Sciences, CMA-Shanghai, Shanghai Jiao Tong University, Shanghai 200240, PR China
a r t i c l e i n f o
Article history:
Received 20 May 2021
Accepted 21 December 2021
Available online 1 January 2022
Communicated by B. Feng
Keywords:
Hirota’s bilinear method
Breather
Algebraic soliton
Lump soliton
Dispersive long wave system
a b s t r a c t
In this paper, we investigate a (2+1)-dimensional dispersive long wave system with an arbitrary con-
stant a. Solitons, line breathers, rational solutions and algebraic solitons of the system are constructed
based on Hirota’s bilinear method and Kadomtsev–Petviashvili (KP) hierarchy reduction technique.
These solutions are expressed in terms of N × N determinant. When the size of the determinant
N is odd, solutions on periodic backgrounds are generated. Solutions on constant backgrounds are
derived when N is even. By using asymptotic analysis, we elucidate explicit expressions of asymptotic
algebraic solitons localized in the straight for the algebraic soliton solutions. As a byproduct, we find
lump solutions for an intermediate potential of the system. The effects of constant a are discussed and
it is shown that the constant can affect collision of solitons. Dynamics of the obtained solutions are
discussed with plots.
© 2021 Elsevier B.V. All rights reserved.
1. Introduction
In the 1960s, Zabusky and Kruskal used finite difference approach to study numerical solutions of KdV equation and introduced
the concept of solitons [1]. Solitons have the prototype elastic collision property, that is, the shapes and velocities keep the same after
collisions. Besides KdV equation, solitons exist in other (1+1)-dimensional continuous and discrete equations, including the modified
KdV, the Burgers equation, the sine–Gordon equation, the nonlinear Schrödinger equation and their higher dimensional extensions.
These equations have been widely used to model nonlinear phenomena in physics, optics, biology and chemistry. Studies on solitons
are very active and the topic has attracted great attention. With the development of soliton theory, several types of soliton solutions
are found, such as simple line solitons, loop solitons, algebraic solitons, breathers, rogue waves, cuspon and peakon solutions, et al. For
(2 + 1)-dimensional integrable equations, for example, Davey–Stewartson (DS) equation and KP equation, there exist localized soliton
solutions, including dromions, lumps and rogue wave solutions. Although mathematical structures and hidden algebraic properties of
soliton equations have attracted more and more attention, investigation of solutions of nonlinear soliton equations is a permanent topic
because these solutions can help us understand nonlinear phenomena.
The structure of soliton equations has been clarified due to various analytic and algebraic methods, such as inverse scattering
method [2], Riemann–Hilbert method [3], Darboux transformation method [4] and Hirota bilinear method [5]. Among those methods,
Hirota bilinear method is purely algebraic and direct. By transforming soliton equations into bilinear forms, we can not only derive
N-soliton solutions and N−periodic wave solutions, but also obtain integrable characteristics such as Bäcklund transformation, Lax
pairs and infinite conservation laws. In general, these soliton solutions own determinant or Pfaffian structures. Sato theory gives deep
understanding of Hirota’s method and present bilinear integrable hierarchy [6]. Recently, breathers and rogue waves have attracted
much attention. There are two kinds of breathers, spacial periodic and time periodic, respectively. In the limit case, breathers could
become rogue waves. Rogue waves originate from oceanography [7] and describe an instantaneous excitation phenomenon. Rogue
wave solutions of integrable systems are rational in both space and time variables. Based on Lax pairs, Darboux transformation method
is useful to construct rational solutions [8–10]. Besides, the KP hierarchy reduction technique based on Hirota bilinear method is also
a powerful approach to generate rational solutions of integrable systems [11–14]. This method has been successfully applied to study
solutions of nonlocal integrable systems [15,16]. Algebraic solitons are some kind of rational solutions and less studied for soliton
∗ Corresponding author.
E-mail address: gfyu@sjtu.edu.cn (G.-F. Yu).
https://doi.org/10.1016/j.physd.2021.133140
0167-2789/© 2021 Elsevier B.V. All rights reserved.
PAGE 2
H.-H. Sheng and G.-F. Yu Physica D 432 (2022) 133140
equations. They appear in KP equation [17], DS equation [18], the Benjamin–Ono equation [19], a model equation for deep water
waves [20], and also in some nonlocal integrable systems [21,22]. Algebraic solitons also exhibit elastic collision phenomena. It is
interesting to find algebraic soliton solutions for soliton equations. Meanwhile, solutions of integrable systems are mainly on constant
backgrounds, zero or nonzero, and less discussion about solutions on the periodic backgrounds.
The aim of the present paper is devoted to the studies of a (2+1)-dimensional dispersive long wave (DLW) system
uyt + vxx + uuxy + uxuy + 2auxy = 0, (1)
vt + (uv)x + uxxy + 2avx + 2λux = 0, (2)
where λ and a are arbitrary constants. By the variable transformation x → x − 2at, DLW system (1)–(2) reduces to
uyt + vxx + uuxy + uxuy = 0, (3)
vt + (uv)x + uxxy + 2λux = 0, (4)
which was firstly derived by Boiti in 1987 [23] to model the hydrodynamics of a wide channel or open sea with a finite depth. Here
u(x, y, t) is the horizontal velocity and v(x, y, t) is the wave height above the undisturbed water surface. Variables t and (x, y) represent
the time and propagation plane, respectively. Under the dimension reduction y = x, one dimensional DLW system can approximate
the inviscid gravity waves on a water layer with constant depth [24,25].
DLW system has attracted great attention. In [26], Grammian and Wronskian type determinant solutions of DLW system were
presented based on the KP reduction approach. By choosing exponential type determinant elements, N−bright soliton solutions of
DLW were derived [26]. Group theoretical analysis [27], non-Painlevé property [28], Darboux transformation [29,30], Kac–Moody–
Virasoro symmetry algebra [31] and line solitons and dromion solutions [32–34] of DLW system were studied. We will present different
kinds of solutions of DLW system and show that soliton solutions for DLW system given in [34] is a constrained case of our soliton
solutions derived here. Recently, Grinevich and Santini [35] show that the rogue wave solution or the Akhmediev breather solution
on a nonzero background expressed by elliptic function can be formed. Breather and rogue wave solutions for the Hirota equation
and nonlinear Schrödinger equation on the elliptic function background were studied [36,37]. The construction of soliton solutions
on periodic function backgrounds is much more challenge. In this paper, one aim is to construct general solutions, such as solitons,
breathers and algebraic soliton solutions, of DLW system on the periodic function background systematically. Compared with asymptotic
analysis of exponential-type solitons, asymptotic behavior of algebraic soliton solutions is much more difficult to be conducted. We
analyze the asymptotic behaviors of algebraic solitons of DLW system and show that wave peaks evolve along the lines.
The paper is organized as follows. In Section 2, we introduce the derivation and reduction of the solutions of DLW system from
the modified KP equations. In Section 3, we analyze the dynamics of solitons on constant and periodic background, respectively. In
Section 4, we investigate the breather solutions. In Section 5, we discuss the dynamic behaviors of the rational solutions which include
algebraic solitons, multiple and high-order rational solutions. Lump solutions are found for some intermediate potential. Section 6 is
devoted to the conclusion and discussion.
2. Soliton and (semi-)rational solutions in determinant form
The spirit of Hirota bilinear method is to transform nonlinear equations into bilinear form by dependent variable transformation.
Firstly, we make the dependent variable transformation
u = 2
(
ln f
g
)
x
, v = 2(ln fg)xy, (5)
and transform DLW system (1)–(2) into the bilinear equations
[Dy(D2
x + Dt + 2aDx) + 2λDx]f · g = 0, (6)
(D2
x + Dt + 2aDx)f · g = 0. (7)
The Hirota’s bilinear operator D is defined by
Dn
x f (x) · g(x) = ∂ n
∂δ n f (x + δ)g(x − δ)|δ=0. (8)
We note that bilinear DLW system is very similar to bilinear (2 + 1)-dimensional sinh-Gordon equation . In fact, under the variable
interchange y ↔ t and a = 0, λ = 1, the bilinear DLW system becomes bilinear (2 + 1)-dimensional sinh-Gordon equation [38,39]. It is
common that different nonlinear integrable equations can share same bilinear equations by different dependent variable transformation.
For example, the bilinear form of the (2 +1)-dimensional Gardner equation is the same as the bilinear modified KP equation [40]. In [26],
bright soliton solutions with the determinant structure were derived for (2 + 1)-dimensional sinh-Gordon equation. Rational solutions
on the zero background with the determinant structure for DLW system were presented in [39].
In the following discussion we take the parameter λ = −2 for convenient. We start from the bilinear modified KP equations

3. Dynamics of the soliton solutions
In this part, we study the dynamics of soliton solutions u and v of DLW system with constant and periodic background, respectively.
First, we elucidate the difference between soliton solutions for DLW system here and soliton solutions for DLW system obtained
in [26,34].
(1) Setting a = 0, cj = 1, the determinant solution of DLW with elements (13)–(15) is reduced to soliton solutions in [26].
(2) Setting a = 0, cj = 1, variables transformation pi = − ˆpi, qj = ˆqj, and the constraint exp( ξi0 + ηj0) = (ˆpi + ˆqj)ˆqj, the determinant
solution of DLW with elements (13)–(15) is degenerated into soliton solutions in [34] with variables ˆp, ˆq.

The asymptotic analysis shows that the collision of two solitons is completely elastic. In other words, shapes and velocities of two
solitary waves keep unchanged after the collision. The collision produces phase shifts θ for Soliton 1 and −θ for Soliton 2, where

solitons with one above the horizon and the other under the horizon. Fig. 5 shows the interaction between two solitons that are both
under the horizon.

6. Conclusion and discussion
The KP hierarchy reduction technique is a powerful method to investigate structures of solutions of integrable systems. Based on
this approach, we investigated a (2+1)-dimensional generalized dispersive wave system. General soliton and (semi-)rational solutions
are given in terms of N ×N determinant. Under different size of the determinant solution, we construct soliton solutions, line breathers,
algebraic solitons, multiple and high-order rational solutions on constant and periodic background, respectively. Dynamics of obtained
solutions with different parameters are analyzed. Roles of constant a in DLW are discussed. As a byproduct, we find lump solutions for
the intermediate potential of DLW system. It is known that the NLS equation possess multi-breather solutions on the elliptic function
background. One question is how to construct breather solutions on the elliptic function background for DLW system. Meanwhile,
nonlocal integrable equations, such as nonlocal NLS and nonlocal mKdV, were proposed from the reduction of well known AKNS system.
These nonlocal systems possess PT symmetries and singular or nonsingular soliton solutions. High dimensional nonlocal integrable
equations, for example, (2+1)-dimensional Davey–Stewartson equation, were also proposed. It would be interesting to investigate
nonlocal DLW system and it remains a topic to be explored in the future.

Solitons, breathers and rational solutions for a (2+1)-dimensional
dispersive long wave system
Han-Han Sheng, Guo-Fu Yu ∗
School of Mathematical Sciences, CMA-Shanghai, Shanghai Jiao Tong University, Shanghai 200240, PR China
a r t i c l e i n f o
Article history:
Received 20 May 2021
Accepted 21 December 2021
Available online 1 January 2022
Communicated by B. Feng
Keywords:
Hirota’s bilinear method
Breather
Algebraic soliton
Lump soliton
Dispersive long wave system
a b s t r a c t
In this paper, we investigate a (2+1)-dimensional dispersive long wave system with an arbitrary con-
stant a. Solitons, line breathers, rational solutions and algebraic solitons of the system are constructed
based on Hirota’s bilinear method and Kadomtsev–Petviashvili (KP) hierarchy reduction technique.
These solutions are expressed in terms of N × N determinant. When the size of the determinant
N is odd, solutions on periodic backgrounds are generated. Solutions on constant backgrounds are
derived when N is even. By using asymptotic analysis, we elucidate explicit expressions of asymptotic
algebraic solitons localized in the straight for the algebraic soliton solutions. As a byproduct, we find
lump solutions for an intermediate potential of the system. The effects of constant a are discussed and
it is shown that the constant can affect collision of solitons. Dynamics of the obtained solutions are
discussed with plots.
© 2021 Elsevier B.V. All rights reserved.
1. Introduction
In the 1960s, Zabusky and Kruskal used finite difference approach to study numerical solutions of KdV equation and introduced
the concept of solitons [1]. Solitons have the prototype elastic collision property, that is, the shapes and velocities keep the same after
collisions. Besides KdV equation, solitons exist in other (1+1)-dimensional continuous and discrete equations, including the modified
KdV, the Burgers equation, the sine–Gordon equation, the nonlinear Schrödinger equation and their higher dimensional extensions.
These equations have been widely used to model nonlinear phenomena in physics, optics, biology and chemistry. Studies on solitons
are very active and the topic has attracted great attention. With the development of soliton theory, several types of soliton solutions
are found, such as simple line solitons, loop solitons, algebraic solitons, breathers, rogue waves, cuspon and peakon solutions, et al. For
(2 + 1)-dimensional integrable equations, for example, Davey–Stewartson (DS) equation and KP equation, there exist localized soliton
solutions, including dromions, lumps and rogue wave solutions. Although mathematical structures and hidden algebraic properties of
soliton equations have attracted more and more attention, investigation of solutions of nonlinear soliton equations is a permanent topic
because these solutions can help us understand nonlinear phenomena.
The structure of soliton equations has been clarified due to various analytic and algebraic methods, such as inverse scattering
method [2], Riemann–Hilbert method [3], Darboux transformation method [4] and Hirota bilinear method [5]. Among those methods,
Hirota bilinear method is purely algebraic and direct. By transforming soliton equations into bilinear forms, we can not only derive
N-soliton solutions and N−periodic wave solutions, but also obtain integrable characteristics such as Bäcklund transformation, Lax
pairs and infinite conservation laws. In general, these soliton solutions own determinant or Pfaffian structures. Sato theory gives deep
understanding of Hirota’s method and present bilinear integrable hierarchy [6]. Recently, breathers and rogue waves have attracted
much attention. There are two kinds of breathers, spacial periodic and time periodic, respectively. In the limit case, breathers could
become rogue waves. Rogue waves originate from oceanography [7] and describe an instantaneous excitation phenomenon. Rogue
wave solutions of integrable systems are rational in both space and time variables. Based on Lax pairs, Darboux transformation method
is useful to construct rational solutions [8–10]. Besides, the KP hierarchy reduction technique based on Hirota bilinear method is also
a powerful approach to generate rational solutions of integrable systems [11–14]. This method has been successfully applied to study
solutions of nonlocal integrable systems [15,16]. Algebraic solitons are some kind of rational solutions and less studied for soliton
∗ Corresponding author.
E-mail address: gfyu@sjtu.edu.cn (G.-F. Yu).
https://doi.org/10.1016/j.physd.2021.133140
0167-2789/© 2021 Elsevier B.V. All rights reserved.
PAGE 2
H.-H. Sheng and G.-F. Yu Physica D 432 (2022) 133140
equations. They appear in KP equation [17], DS equation [18], the Benjamin–Ono equation [19], a model equation for deep water
waves [20], and also in some nonlocal integrable systems [21,22]. Algebraic solitons also exhibit elastic collision phenomena. It is
interesting to find algebraic soliton solutions for soliton equations. Meanwhile, solutions of integrable systems are mainly on constant
backgrounds, zero or nonzero, and less discussion about solutions on the periodic backgrounds.
The aim of the present paper is devoted to the studies of a (2+1)-dimensional dispersive long wave (DLW) system
uyt + vxx + uuxy + uxuy + 2auxy = 0, (1)
vt + (uv)x + uxxy + 2avx + 2λux = 0, (2)
where λ and a are arbitrary constants. By the variable transformation x → x − 2at, DLW system (1)–(2) reduces to
uyt + vxx + uuxy + uxuy = 0, (3)
vt + (uv)x + uxxy + 2λux = 0, (4)
which was firstly derived by Boiti in 1987 [23] to model the hydrodynamics of a wide channel or open sea with a finite depth. Here
u(x, y, t) is the horizontal velocity and v(x, y, t) is the wave height above the undisturbed water surface. Variables t and (x, y) represent
the time and propagation plane, respectively. Under the dimension reduction y = x, one dimensional DLW system can approximate
the inviscid gravity waves on a water layer with constant depth [24,25].
DLW system has attracted great attention. In [26], Grammian and Wronskian type determinant solutions of DLW system were
presented based on the KP reduction approach. By choosing exponential type determinant elements, N−bright soliton solutions of
DLW were derived [26]. Group theoretical analysis [27], non-Painlevé property [28], Darboux transformation [29,30], Kac–Moody–
Virasoro symmetry algebra [31] and line solitons and dromion solutions [32–34] of DLW system were studied. We will present different
kinds of solutions of DLW system and show that soliton solutions for DLW system given in [34] is a constrained case of our soliton
solutions derived here. Recently, Grinevich and Santini [35] show that the rogue wave solution or the Akhmediev breather solution
on a nonzero background expressed by elliptic function can be formed. Breather and rogue wave solutions for the Hirota equation
and nonlinear Schrödinger equation on the elliptic function background were studied [36,37]. The construction of soliton solutions
on periodic function backgrounds is much more challenge. In this paper, one aim is to construct general solutions, such as solitons,
breathers and algebraic soliton solutions, of DLW system on the periodic function background systematically. Compared with asymptotic
analysis of exponential-type solitons, asymptotic behavior of algebraic soliton solutions is much more difficult to be conducted. We
analyze the asymptotic behaviors of algebraic solitons of DLW system and show that wave peaks evolve along the lines.
The paper is organized as follows. In Section 2, we introduce the derivation and reduction of the solutions of DLW system from
the modified KP equations. In Section 3, we analyze the dynamics of solitons on constant and periodic background, respectively. In
Section 4, we investigate the breather solutions. In Section 5, we discuss the dynamic behaviors of the rational solutions which include
algebraic solitons, multiple and high-order rational solutions. Lump solutions are found for some intermediate potential. Section 6 is
devoted to the conclusion and discussion.
2. Soliton and (semi-)rational solutions in determinant form
The spirit of Hirota bilinear method is to transform nonlinear equations into bilinear form by dependent variable transformation.
Firstly, we make the dependent variable transformation
u = 2
(
ln f
g
)
x
, v = 2(ln fg)xy, (5)
and transform DLW system (1)–(2) into the bilinear equations
[Dy(D2
x + Dt + 2aDx) + 2λDx]f · g = 0, (6)
(D2
x + Dt + 2aDx)f · g = 0. (7)
The Hirota’s bilinear operator D is defined by
Dn
x f (x) · g(x) = ∂ n
∂δ n f (x + δ)g(x − δ)|δ=0. (8)
We note that bilinear DLW system is very similar to bilinear (2 + 1)-dimensional sinh-Gordon equation . In fact, under the variable
interchange y ↔ t and a = 0, λ = 1, the bilinear DLW system becomes bilinear (2 + 1)-dimensional sinh-Gordon equation [38,39]. It is
common that different nonlinear integrable equations can share same bilinear equations by different dependent variable transformation.
For example, the bilinear form of the (2 +1)-dimensional Gardner equation is the same as the bilinear modified KP equation [40]. In [26],
bright soliton solutions with the determinant structure were derived for (2 + 1)-dimensional sinh-Gordon equation. Rational solutions
on the zero background with the determinant structure for DLW system were presented in [39].

3. Dynamics of the soliton solutions
In this part, we study the dynamics of soliton solutions u and v of DLW system with constant and periodic background, respectively.
First, we elucidate the difference between soliton solutions for DLW system here and soliton solutions for DLW system obtained
in [26,34].
(1) Setting a = 0, cj = 1, the determinant solution of DLW with elements (13)–(15) is reduced to soliton solutions in [26].
(2) Setting a = 0, cj = 1, variables transformation pi = − ˆpi, qj = ˆqj, and the constraint exp( ξi0 + ηj0) = (ˆpi + ˆqj)ˆqj, the determinant
solution of DLW with elements (13)–(15) is degenerated into soliton solutions in [34] with variables ˆp, ˆq.

6. Conclusion and discussion
The KP hierarchy reduction technique is a powerful method to investigate structures of solutions of integrable systems. Based on
this approach, we investigated a (2+1)-dimensional generalized dispersive wave system. General soliton and (semi-)rational solutions
are given in terms of N ×N determinant. Under different size of the determinant solution, we construct soliton solutions, line breathers,
algebraic solitons, multiple and high-order rational solutions on constant and periodic background, respectively. Dynamics of obtained
solutions with different parameters are analyzed. Roles of constant a in DLW are discussed. As a byproduct, we find lump solutions for
the intermediate potential of DLW system. It is known that the NLS equation possess multi-breather solutions on the elliptic function
background. One question is how to construct breather solutions on the elliptic function background for DLW system. Meanwhile,
nonlocal integrable equations, such as nonlocal NLS and nonlocal mKdV, were proposed from the reduction of well known AKNS system.
These nonlocal systems possess PT symmetries and singular or nonsingular soliton solutions. High dimensional nonlocal integrable
equations, for example, (2+1)-dimensional Davey–Stewartson equation, were also proposed. It would be interesting to investigate
nonlocal DLW system and it remains a topic to be explored in the future.

H.-H. Sheng and G.-F. Yu Physica D 432 (2022) 133140

Introduction\\r\\nIn the 1960s, Zabusky and Kruskal used finite difference approach to study numerical solutions of KdV equation and introduced\\r\\nthe concept of solitons [1]. Solitons have the prototype elastic collision property, that is, the shapes and velocities keep the same after\\r\\ncollisions. Besides KdV equation, solitons exist in other (1+1)-dimensional continuous and discrete equations, including the modified\\r\\nKdV, the Burgers equation, the sine–Gordon equation, the nonlinear Schrödinger equation and their higher dimensional extensions.\\r\\nThese equations have been widely used to model nonlinear phenomena in physics, optics, biology and chemistry. Studies on solitons\\r\\nare very active and the topic has attracted great attention. With the development of soliton theory, several types of soliton solutions\\r\\nare found, such as simple line solitons, loop solitons, algebraic solitons, breathers, rogue waves, cuspon and peakon solutions, et al. For\\r\\n(2 + 1)-dimensional integrable equations, for example, Davey–Stewartson (DS) equation and KP equation, there exist localized soliton\\r\\nsolutions, including dromions, lumps and rogue wave solutions. Although mathematical structures and hidden algebraic properties of\\r\\nsoliton equations have attracted more and more attention, investigation of solutions of nonlinear soliton equations is a permanent topic\\r\\nbecause these solutions can help us understand nonlinear phenomena.\\r\\nThe structure of soliton equations has been clarified due to various analytic and algebraic methods, such as inverse scattering\\r\\nmethod [2], Riemann–Hilbert method [3], Darboux transformation method [4] and Hirota bilinear method [5]. Among those methods,\\r\\nHirota bilinear method is purely algebraic and direct. By transforming soliton equations into bilinear forms, we can not only derive\\r\\nN-soliton solutions and N−periodic wave solutions, but also obtain integrable characteristics such as Bäcklund transformation, Lax\\r\\npairs and infinite conservation laws. In general, these soliton solutions own determinant or Pfaffian structures. Sato theory gives deep\\r\\nunderstanding of Hirota’s method and present bilinear integrable hierarchy [6]. Recently, breathers and rogue waves have attracted\\r\\nmuch attention. There are two kinds of breathers, spacial periodic and time periodic, respectively. In the limit case, breathers could\\r\\nbecome rogue waves. Rogue waves originate from oceanography [7] and describe an instantaneous excitation phenomenon. Rogue\\r\\nwave solutions of integrable systems are rational in both space and time variables. Based on Lax pairs, Darboux transformation method\\r\\nis useful to construct rational solutions [8–10]. Besides, the KP hierarchy reduction technique based on Hirota bilinear method is also\\r\\na powerful approach to generate rational solutions of integrable systems [11–14]. This method has been successfully applied to study\\r\\nsolutions of nonlocal integrable systems [15,16]. Algebraic solitons are some kind of rational solutions and less studied for soliton\\r\\n∗ Corresponding author.\\r\\nE-mail address: gfyu@sjtu.edu.cn (G.-F. Yu).\\r\\nhttps://doi.org/10.1016/j.physd.2021.133140\\r\\n0167-2789/© 2021 Elsevier B.V. All rights reserved.\\r\\nPAGE 2\\r\\nH.-H. Sheng and G.-F. Yu Physica D 432 (2022) 133140\\r\\nequations. They appear in KP equation [17], DS equation [18], the Benjamin–Ono equation [19], a model equation for deep water\\r\\nwaves [20], and also in some nonlocal integrable systems [21,22]. Algebraic solitons also exhibit elastic collision phenomena. It is\\r\\ninteresting to find algebraic soliton solutions for soliton equations. Meanwhile, solutions of integrable systems are mainly on constant\\r\\nbackgrounds, zero or nonzero, and less discussion about solutions on the periodic backgrounds.\\r\\nThe aim of the present paper is devoted to th

############ ord 61  type=response_item  2026-09-17T06:25:39.321Z ############
  [response_item.type = message]

----- MSG[assistant].output_text -----
我看了这篇文章，**你的思路可以发展成一个研究项目，但建议把“可积离散化”和“数值稳定性分析”分成两条相互验证的线。** 一个关键发现是：这个方程按物理时间 \(t\) 演化时，存在高频不稳定的问题。因此，“得到半离散可积系统”很有希望；“进一步证明一般初值问题的稳定性和收敛阶”需要先明确适用条件，不能直接承诺。

论文是 Sheng、Yu 的 *Solitons, breathers and rational solutions for a (2+1)-dimensional dispersive long wave system*，发表于 Physica D 432 (2022)。下面依据其第 2—3 页的方程和行列式结构展开。:codex-file-citation{path="C:/Users/msz/学术内容/PhysD-published.pdf" purpose="source"}

**1. 我们的起点已经是双线性方程**

原非线性系统是
\[
u_{yt}+v_{xx}+u u_{xy}+u_xu_y+2au_{xy}=0,
\]
\[
v_t+(uv)_x+u_{xxy}+2av_x+2\lambda u_x=0.
\]

文章通过第 (5) 式
\[
u=2\left(\ln\frac fg\right)_x,\qquad
v=2(\ln fg)_{xy}
\]
得到你说的第 (6)、(7) 式：
\[
\big[D_y(D_x^2+D_t+2aD_x)+2\lambda D_x\big]f\cdot g=0,
\]
\[
(D_x^2+D_t+2aD_x)f\cdot g=0.
\]

所以，建议的路线是
\[
\boxed{
\text{已有连续双线性系统}
\longrightarrow
\text{\(y\) 方向半离散双线性系统}
\longrightarrow
\text{离散变量变换}
\longrightarrow
\text{半离散非线性系统}
}
\]
然后分别检查它的可积结构、连续极限和数值性质。

这里把你说的 VT 理解为 variable transformation。只离散 \(y\)，保留 \(x,t\) 连续，最终得到的是微分—差分系统；真正做计算时，还需要处理 \(x\) 和时间推进。

**2. 先做一个决定研究方向的检查：连续方程是否适合一般初值计算**

我对常数背景
\[
u=u_0,\qquad v=v_0
\]
做了线性化。令扰动为
\[
(U,V)e^{st+i(kx+\ell y)},\qquad \ell\ne0,
\]
记 \(c=u_0+2a\)，得到
\[
i\ell(s+ick)U-k^2V=0,
\]
\[
(s+ick)V+ik(v_0+2\lambda-k\ell)U=0.
\]
因此
\[
\boxed{
(s+ick)^2=k^4-(v_0+2\lambda)\frac{k^3}{\ell}.
}
\]

固定非零 \(\ell\)，当 \(|k|\to\infty\)，有一个分支满足
\[
\operatorname{Re}s\sim k^2>0.
\]

**这意味着：在通常允许任意高频扰动的周期或全空间问题中，线性化系统不具有一般 Sobolev 范数下的统一时间稳定估计。** 这不是换一个时间积分器就能解决的数值误差，而是连续模型本身的高频增长。

另外，\(\ell=0\) 不能通过除以 \(\ell\) 处理。由于第一式含 \(u_{yt}\)，\(y\) 平均模态涉及约束和额外的闭合条件。

这对项目的影响很具体：

- 可以研究精确解、可积离散结构，以及特定解族的连续极限。
- 可以研究固定频带、有限分辨率和有限时间内的误差增长。
- 如果要证明一般数值解收敛，需要先建立适当的问题设定或引入明确的正则化，并说明正则化改变了什么。
- 不能直接套用“相容性＋稳定性＝收敛性”；非线性问题本来就需要额外估计，这里还有连续适定性的障碍。

我建议把这个检查放在项目第一阶段，而不是最后出现数值爆炸时再处理。

**3. 半离散双线性系统怎么构造：先有基线，再追求可积结构**

令
\[
y_j=jh,\qquad f_j(x,t)\approx f(x,y_j,t),\quad
g_j(x,t)\approx g(x,y_j,t),
\]
并记
\[
B=D_x^2+D_t+2aD_x.
\]

最直接的试验候选是
\[
Bf_j\cdot g_j=0,
\]
\[
\frac{Bf_{j+1}\cdot g_{j-1}-Bf_{j-1}\cdot g_{j+1}}{2h}
+2\lambda D_xf_j\cdot g_j=0.
\]

它对应于形式替换
\[
D_y\longmapsto\frac{\sinh(hD_y)}{h}
=D_y+\frac{h^2}{6}D_y^3+O(h^4).
\]

因此，对足够光滑的连续 \(f,g\) 采样，它具有双线性方程层面的 \(O(h^2)\) 残差。**但它目前只是一套形式相容的候选，不能据此认定它可积、具有良好的初值问题，或者还原后的 \(u,v\) 有二阶误差。**

尤其要注意：
\[
D_yBf\cdot g
\]
不是对普通函数 \(Bf\cdot g\) 求一次 \(y\) 导数。所以不能因为 \(Bf\cdot g=0\)，就把第 (6) 式的第一项消掉。

真正值得重点投入的是论文现成的 **modified KP 层级结构**。文章已经给出
\[
x_{-1}=y,\qquad x_1=x,\qquad x_2=-t,
\]
以及
\[
f=\tau_{n+1},\qquad g=\tau_n.
\]

可以沿着这个结构做：

1. 在对应的层级中寻找离散负流、Miwa 移位或双线性 Bäcklund 关系。
2. 引入代表 \(y\) 网格的独立指标 \(j\)，得到 \(\tau_n(j)\)。
3. 推导相邻格点的双线性恒等式，再做约化。
4. 验证连续极限确实回到第 (6)、(7) 式。

这里有两个容易踩的坑：

- 原文的 \(n\) 是 tau 函数的层级指标，**不能直接把它当作 \(y\) 网格指标**。
- Miwa 移位通常同时涉及多个层级时间，必须验证约化后得到的确实是目标 \(y\) 离散化。

建议先用单孤子、双孤子筛选候选关系，最后用行列式恒等式、Bäcklund 结构或 Lax 表示提供更强的结构证据。仅有一个孤子解并不足以说明可积。

**4. 从半离散双线性方程回到非线性方程，重点是“闭合”**

可以先引入
\[
q_j=\ln\frac{f_j}{g_j},\qquad
r_j=\ln(f_jg_j).
\]
连续变量满足
\[
u=2q_x,\qquad v=2r_{xy}.
\]

离散时，一个自然的尝试是
\[
u_j=2q_{j,x},\qquad
v_{j+1/2}=\frac{2}{h}\partial_x(r_{j+1}-r_j).
\]

这个 \(v\) 位于网格中点；在中点意义下，它对光滑函数可以有二阶近似。但最终是否采用它，应由推导出的双线性格点关系决定。

这里的验收标准不是“写出类似原方程的式子”，而是：

- 能否只用选定的非线性变量及有限个邻点表达方程？
- 若出现额外的 tau 比值，是否需要保留一个辅助变量？
- 变换是否引入积分常数、规范自由度或边界约束？
- 在 \(f_j,g_j\ne0\) 的适当区域内，两个系统如何对应？
- 连续极限能否逐项恢复原系统？

**不必强求最终只有 \(u_j,v_j\) 两个变量。** 如果保留一个辅助场能得到局部、清楚、可验证的系统，通常比强行消元得到非局部表达式更合适。

**5. 数值分析应该拆成三种不同的问题**

| 层次 | 具体要回答的问题 | 可以得到的结果 |
|---|---|---|
| 连续极限与相容性 | 连续解代入半离散系统，残差是多少？ | \(O(h^p)\) 的截断误差 |
| 离散精确解的极限 | 离散孤子是否趋向连续孤子？ | 特定解族的误差及相位偏差 |
| 初值求解的稳定与收敛 | 扰动是否受控？误差是否随网格细化下降？ | 需要适定性和统一稳定估计的数值结论 |

这三件事不能互相替代。**精确解的图像吻合，不等于一般初值算法收敛；方程残差二阶，也不自动等于数值解二阶。**

第一轮实验建议只做：

1. **光滑无奇点单孤子**：检测残差阶、振幅误差和相位误差。
2. **小幅 Fourier 扰动**：比较实际增长率与上面的线性色散关系，识别连续不稳定和额外数值不稳定。
3. **双孤子相互作用**：在前两项通过后，比较碰撞后的相位偏移。

暂时不从高阶有理解或复杂周期背景开始：它们更容易受到 tau 零点、边界误差和高频增长的影响。

计算时可以用 Python 的 NumPy/SciPy；在匹配的周期条件下用 Fourier 方法处理 \(x\)，时间推进根据线性谱选择。研究 \(y\) 方向误差时，必须单独控制 \(x\) 离散误差、时间误差和边界截断误差。这里不预先承诺某种积分器能消除模型本身的增长。

**6. 我建议的执行顺序与阶段成果**

| 阶段 | 工作 | 通过标准 |
|---|---|---|
| 第一阶段：问题审计 | 核对双线性变换、线性色散、零模态和边界条件 | 明确研究哪种解、哪种演化问题 |
| 第二阶段：候选构造 | 建立直接差分基线；沿 mKP 结构推导半离散关系 | 有明确格点方程及正确连续极限 |
| 第三阶段：结构与闭合 | 构造孤子解、推导离散 VT、还原非线性系统 | 得到闭合系统和结构证明 |
| 第四阶段：误差研究 | 推导截断误差、精确解误差、离散色散关系 | 明确哪些阶数已证明、哪些仅实验支持 |
| 第五阶段：数值验证 | 单孤子、扰动增长、双孤子实验 | 可重复的误差表和适用范围 |

所需知识和工具可以控制在一个比较集中的范围：

- **代数构造**：Hirota 算子、tau 函数、Bäcklund 变换、KP/mKP 层级、行列式恒等式。
- **分析**：Taylor 展开、Fourier 符号、适定性、离散能量估计和非线性误差估计。
- **符号计算**：Mathematica、Maple 或 SymPy，用于核对展开与候选恒等式。
- **数值计算**：NumPy、SciPy、Matplotlib；接近 tau 零点时再考虑高精度计算。

论文后续取了 \(\lambda=-2\)。第一轮可以沿用这个值以直接利用文中的解，保留 \(a\) 做符号推导；数值验证先取 \(a=0\) 简化平移效应。

**我更推荐的首个研究目标是：构造与 mKP 结构相容的 \(y\) 半离散 DLW 系统，得到闭合的非线性形式，并分析连续极限、孤子误差和线性谱。** 这个目标比一开始承诺一般非线性收敛定理更扎实，也更容易形成完整成果。

目前可以确认的是：双线性起点和层级入口都很明确；直接差分基线可以立即展开；可积半离散关系与非线性闭合仍需推导。至于新颖性，还需要检索已有的半离散 DLW、相关 sinh-Gordon 和 mKP 负流工作，判断我们得到的是否是已有系统的变量变换。

- :codex-followup[先核对连续结构与适定性]{prompt="请完整推导 PhysD-published.pdf 的双线性变换、常数背景线性色散关系和 y 零模态约束，明确适合后续数值分析的问题设定。"}
- :codex-followup[开始推导半离散系统]{prompt="请从论文的 modified KP 和 tau 行列式结构出发，尝试推导 y 方向的半离散双线性系统，区分候选公式与已验证结论，并检查连续极限。"}
- :codex-followup[检索已有工作并定位创新]{prompt="请检索半离散 DLW、相关 (2+1) 维 sinh-Gordon 和 modified KP 负流的已有工作，比较与本文方程的对应关系，给出有来源依据的创新定位。"}

  [event_msg.type = task_complete]

----- EVT.task_complete.last_agent_message -----
我看了这篇文章，**你的思路可以发展成一个研究项目，但建议把“可积离散化”和“数值稳定性分析”分成两条相互验证的线。** 一个关键发现是：这个方程按物理时间 \(t\) 演化时，存在高频不稳定的问题。因此，“得到半离散可积系统”很有希望；“进一步证明一般初值问题的稳定性和收敛阶”需要先明确适用条件，不能直接承诺。

论文是 Sheng、Yu 的 *Solitons, breathers and rational solutions for a (2+1)-dimensional dispersive long wave system*，发表于 Physica D 432 (2022)。下面依据其第 2—3 页的方程和行列式结构展开。:codex-file-citation{path="C:/Users/msz/学术内容/PhysD-published.pdf" purpose="source"}

**1. 我们的起点已经是双线性方程**

原非线性系统是
\[
u_{yt}+v_{xx}+u u_{xy}+u_xu_y+2au_{xy}=0,
\]
\[
v_t+(uv)_x+u_{xxy}+2av_x+2\lambda u_x=0.
\]

文章通过第 (5) 式
\[
u=2\left(\ln\frac fg\right)_x,\qquad
v=2(\ln fg)_{xy}
\]
得到你说的第 (6)、(7) 式：
\[
\big[D_y(D_x^2+D_t+2aD_x)+2\lambda D_x\big]f\cdot g=0,
\]
\[
(D_x^2+D_t+2aD_x)f\cdot g=0.
\]

所以，建议的路线是
\[
\boxed{
\text{已有连续双线性系统}
\longrightarrow
\text{\(y\) 方向半离散双线性系统}
\longrightarrow
\text{离散变量变换}
\longrightarrow
\text{半离散非线性系统}
}
\]
然后分别检查它的可积结构、连续极限和数值性质。

这里把你说的 VT 理解为 variable transformation。只离散 \(y\)，保留 \(x,t\) 连续，最终得到的是微分—差分系统；真正做计算时，还需要处理 \(x\) 和时间推进。

**2. 先做一个决定研究方向的检查：连续方程是否适合一般初值计算**

我对常数背景
\[
u=u_0,\qquad v=v_0
\]
做了线性化。令扰动为
\[
(U,V)e^{st+i(kx+\ell y)},\qquad \ell\ne0,
\]
记 \(c=u_0+2a\)，得到
\[
i\ell(s+ick)U-k^2V=0,
\]
\[
(s+ick)V+ik(v_0+2\lambda-k\ell)U=0.
\]
因此
\[
\boxed{
(s+ick)^2=k^4-(v_0+2\lambda)\frac{k^3}{\ell}.
}
\]

固定非零 \(\ell\)，当 \(|k|\to\infty\)，有一个分支满足
\[
\operatorname{Re}s\sim k^2>0.
\]

**这意味着：在通常允许任意高频扰动的周期或全空间问题中，线性化系统不具有一般 Sobolev 范数下的统一时间稳定估计。** 这不是换一个时间积分器就能解决的数值误差，而是连续模型本身的高频增长。

另外，\(\ell=0\) 不能通过除以 \(\ell\) 处理。由于第一式含 \(u_{yt}\)，\(y\) 平均模态涉及约束和额外的闭合条件。

这对项目的影响很具体：

- 可以研究精确解、可积离散结构，以及特定解族的连续极限。
- 可以研究固定频带、有限分辨率和有限时间内的误差增长。
- 如果要证明一般数值解收敛，需要先建立适当的问题设定或引入明确的正则化，并说明正则化改变了什么。
- 不能直接套用“相容性＋稳定性＝收敛性”；非线性问题本来就需要额外估计，这里还有连续适定性的障碍。

我建议把这个检查放在项目第一阶段，而不是最后出现数值爆炸时再处理。

**3. 半离散双线性系统怎么构造：先有基线，再追求可积结构**

令
\[
y_j=jh,\qquad f_j(x,t)\approx f(x,y_j,t),\quad
g_j(x,t)\approx g(x,y_j,t),
\]
并记
\[
B=D_x^2+D_t+2aD_x.
\]

最直接的试验候选是
\[
Bf_j\cdot g_j=0,
\]
\[
\frac{Bf_{j+1}\cdot g_{j-1}-Bf_{j-1}\cdot g_{j+1}}{2h}
+2\lambda D_xf_j\cdot g_j=0.
\]

它对应于形式替换
\[
D_y\longmapsto\frac{\sinh(hD_y)}{h}
=D_y+\frac{h^2}{6}D_y^3+O(h^4).
\]

因此，对足够光滑的连续 \(f,g\) 采样，它具有双线性方程层面的 \(O(h^2)\) 残差。**但它目前只是一套形式相容的候选，不能据此认定它可积、具有良好的初值问题，或者还原后的 \(u,v\) 有二阶误差。**

尤其要注意：
\[
D_yBf\cdot g
\]
不是对普通函数 \(Bf\cdot g\) 求一次 \(y\) 导数。所以不能因为 \(Bf\cdot g=0\)，就把第 (6) 式的第一项消掉。

真正值得重点投入的是论文现成的 **modified KP 层级结构**。文章已经给出
\[
x_{-1}=y,\qquad x_1=x,\qquad x_2=-t,
\]
以及
\[
f=\tau_{n+1},\qquad g=\tau_n.
\]

可以沿着这个结构做：

1. 在对应的层级中寻找离散负流、Miwa 移位或双线性 Bäcklund 关系。
2. 引入代表 \(y\) 网格的独立指标 \(j\)，得到 \(\tau_n(j)\)。
3. 推导相邻格点的双线性恒等式，再做约化。
4. 验证连续极限确实回到第 (6)、(7) 式。

这里有两个容易踩的坑：

- 原文的 \(n\) 是 tau 函数的层级指标，**不能直接把它当作 \(y\) 网格指标**。
- Miwa 移位通常同时涉及多个层级时间，必须验证约化后得到的确实是目标 \(y\) 离散化。

建议先用单孤子、双孤子筛选候选关系，最后用行列式恒等式、Bäcklund 结构或 Lax 表示提供更强的结构证据。仅有一个孤子解并不足以说明可积。

**4. 从半离散双线性方程回到非线性方程，重点是“闭合”**

可以先引入
\[
q_j=\ln\frac{f_j}{g_j},\qquad
r_j=\ln(f_jg_j).
\]
连续变量满足
\[
u=2q_x,\qquad v=2r_{xy}.
\]

离散时，一个自然的尝试是
\[
u_j=2q_{j,x},\qquad
v_{j+1/2}=\frac{2}{h}\partial_x(r_{j+1}-r_j).
\]

这个 \(v\) 位于网格中点；在中点意义下，它对光滑函数可以有二阶近似。但最终是否采用它，应由推导出的双线性格点关系决定。

这里的验收标准不是“写出类似原方程的式子”，而是：

- 能否只用选定的非线性变量及有限个邻点表达方程？
- 若出现额外的 tau 比值，是否需要保留一个辅助变量？
- 变换是否引入积分常数、规范自由度或边界约束？
- 在 \(f_j,g_j\ne0\) 的适当区域内，两个系统如何对应？
- 连续极限能否逐项恢复原系统？

**不必强求最终只有 \(u_j,v_j\) 两个变量。** 如果保留一个辅助场能得到局部、清楚、可验证的系统，通常比强行消元得到非局部表达式更合适。

**5. 数值分析应该拆成三种不同的问题**

| 层次 | 具体要回答的问题 | 可以得到的结果 |
|---|---|---|
| 连续极限与相容性 | 连续解代入半离散系统，残差是多少？ | \(O(h^p)\) 的截断误差 |
| 离散精确解的极限 | 离散孤子是否趋向连续孤子？ | 特定解族的误差及相位偏差 |
| 初值求解的稳定与收敛 | 扰动是否受控？误差是否随网格细化下降？ | 需要适定性和统一稳定估计的数值结论 |

这三件事不能互相替代。**精确解的图像吻合，不等于一般初值算法收敛；方程残差二阶，也不自动等于数值解二阶。**

第一轮实验建议只做：

暂时不从高阶有理解或复杂周期背景开始：它们更容易受到 tau 零点、边界误差和高频增长的影响。

计算时可以用 Python 的 NumPy/SciPy；在匹配的周期条件下用 Fourier 方法处理 \(x\)，时间推进根据线性谱选择。研究 \(y\) 方向误差时，必须单独控制 \(x\) 离散误差、时间误差和边界截断误差。这里不预先承诺某种积分器能消除模型本身的增长。

import Init.Grind.Ring.Field
import Init.GrindInstances.Ring.Rat

/-!
# A first formal Toda calculation

We use the pointwise form of the bilinear Toda equation

  τₙ τₙ'' - (τₙ')² = τₙ₊₁ τₙ₋₁ - τₙ².

For positive τ-functions, `qₙ = log τₙ` turns its left-hand side into
`τₙ² qₙ''`, while the quotient of neighbouring τ-functions becomes an
exponential.  The theorem below is the algebraic core of that conversion.
-/

open Lean.Grind

/- The exponential expression is kept as a quotient at this first stage.
   Analytically, it is `exp (log τnext + log τprev - 2 log τ)` for positive
   τ-values.  The quotient is the exact algebraic content needed here. -/
def todaGap (τprev τ τnext : Rat) : Rat :=
  (τnext * τprev) / τ ^ 2

theorem bilinear_to_log_toda
    (τprev τ τnext τt τtt : Rat)
    (hτ : τ ≠ 0)
    (hbilin : τ * τtt - τt ^ 2 = τnext * τprev - τ ^ 2) :
    τtt / τ - (τt / τ) ^ 2 = todaGap τprev τ τnext - 1 := by
  simp [todaGap, Field.div_eq_mul_inv]
  grind

/-!
The usual nonlinear semi-discrete Toda equation is obtained by taking the
difference of the preceding identity at two adjacent lattice sites.  This
small lemma records the corresponding cancellation independently of τ.
-/

theorem toda_difference
    (E : Rat → Rat) (qdd_prev qdd q_prev q qnext : Rat)
    (hprev : qdd_prev = E (q - q_prev) - 1)
    (h : qdd = E (qnext - q) - 1) :
    qdd - qdd_prev = E (qnext - q) - E (q - q_prev) := by
  rw [h, hprev]
  grind
-- This module serves as the root of the `TodaFormalization` library.
-- Import modules here that should be built as part of the library.
import Main
import Lake

open Lake DSL

package TodaFormalization

lean_lib TodaFormalization
def hello := "world"
leanprover/lean4:v4.34.0

This\\r\\nsmall lemma records the corresponding cancellation independently of τ.\\r\\n-/\\r\\n\\r\\ntheorem toda_difference\\r\\n    (E : Rat → Rat) (qdd_prev qdd q_prev q qnext : Rat)\\r\\n    (hprev : qdd_prev = E (q - q_prev) - 1)\\r\\n    (h : qdd = E (qnext - q) - 1) :\\r\\n    qdd - qdd_prev = E (qnext - q) - E (q - q_prev) := by\\r\\n  rw [h, hprev]\\r\\n  grind\\r\\n-- This module serves as the root of the `TodaFormalization` library.\\r\\n-- Import modules here that should be built as part of the library.\\r\\nimport Main\\r\\nimport Lake\\r\\n\\r\\nopen Lake DSL\\r\\n\\r\\npackage TodaFormalization\\r\\n\\r\\nlean_lib TodaFormalization\\r\\ndef hello := \\"world\\"\\r\\nleanprover/lean4:v4.34.0\\r\\n"}'}]

a) + 1 / (q[i] + a) for i in range(n)]\n+    R = [(2 + h * e) / (2 - h * e) for e in ell]\n+    g = {}\n+    f = {}\n+    for m in modes:\n+        active = [i for i in range(n) if m[i]]\n+        c = prod((1 / (p[i] + q[i]) for i in active), start=F(1))\n+        for i in active:\n+            for j in active:\n+                if i < j:\n+                    c *= (p[i] - ############ ord 175  type=response_item  2026-09-17T08:58:19.770Z ############
  [response_item.type = message]

----- MSG[assistant].output_text -----
明白，是 **Lean**。我已经使用你文件夹中的项目完成了第一轮代数验证。

**现在得到了一套明确的、沿 \(y\) 方向二阶形式相容的半离散双线性系统。它可以作为第一版构造，但尚不能称为“可积离散化”：一孤子检验通过，原连续双孤子结构直接移植的检验没有通过。** 下面把怎么构造、结果长什么样、适用条件和验证边界说清楚。

**1. 只把 \(y\) 换成格点，保留 \(x,t\) 连续**

记
\[
B=D_x^2+D_t+2aD_x.
\]
原来的两条双线性方程就是
\[
Bf\cdot g=0,\qquad
(D_yB+2\lambda D_x)f\cdot g=0.
\]

取网格
\[
y_j=jh,\qquad h>0,
\]
将未知函数变成
\[
f_j(x,t),\qquad g_j(x,t).
\]

第一条不含 \(y\) 导数，最自然的处理是在每个格点保留：
\[
\boxed{Bf_j\cdot g_j=0.}\tag{SD1}
\]

真正需要构造的是第二条中的 \(D_y\)。

这里不能把 \(D_y\) 当成普通的 \(\partial_y\)，因为
\[
D_yf\cdot g=f_yg-fg_y.
\]
它要求两个因子的变化具有相反符号。

**2. 用相邻格点的交叉乘积近似 Hirota 导数**

考虑
\[
f_{j+1}g_j,\qquad f_jg_{j+1}.
\]

它们分别把第一个因子、第二个因子向前移动一个格点。以中点
\[
Y=y_{j+1/2}
\]
为参照，有
\[
f_{j+1}g_j
=f(Y+h/2)g(Y-h/2),
\]
\[
f_jg_{j+1}
=f(Y-h/2)g(Y+h/2).
\]

因此，反对称组合满足
\[
\frac{f_{j+1}g_j-f_jg_{j+1}}h
=D_yf\cdot g\big|_Y+O(h^2).
\]

对称组合则满足
\[
\frac{f_{j+1}g_j+f_jg_{j+1}}2
=fg\big|_Y+O(h^2).
\]

**两项都放在同一个网格中点，是这个构造保持二阶形式精度的关键。** 如果第一项在中点、另一项却直接取左端点，就不能这样得到二阶结论。

**3. 得到的半离散双线性方程**

由此构造：
\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
&(D_x^2+D_t+2aD_x)f_j\cdot g_j=0,\\[2mm]
&\frac1h(D_x^2+D_t+2aD_x)
\bigl(f_{j+1}\cdot g_j-f_j\cdot g_{j+1}\bigr)\\
&\hspace{15mm}
+\lambda D_x
\bigl(f_{j+1}\cdot g_j+f_j\cdot g_{j+1}\bigr)=0.
\end{aligned}
}\tag{SD}
\]

括号内的记号表示双线性算子分别作用后相加或相减。例如
\[
B(F\cdot G)
=F_{xx}G-2F_xG_x+FG_{xx}
+F_tG-FG_t+2a(F_xG-FG_x).
\]

所以这是一套明确的微分—差分方程：

- \(y\) 方向只涉及相邻格点 \(j,j+1\)；
- \(x\) 方向仍有连续的一、二阶导数；
- \(t\) 方向仍为连续的一阶导数；
- 每一项仍是两个未知函数或其导数的乘积，因此保留双线性形式。

论文后续使用 \(\lambda=-2\)，此时第二条中的 \(\lambda D_x(\cdots)\) 就是 \(-2D_x(\cdots)\)。

**4. 为什么它能回到原来的方程？**

利用形式移位记号，第二条在网格中点对应
\[
\left[
\frac2h\sinh\!\left(\frac h2D_y\right)B
+2\lambda\cosh\!\left(\frac h2D_y\right)D_x
\right]f\cdot g=0.
\]

展开得到
\[
\begin{aligned}
&D_yB+2\lambda D_x\\
&\quad+h^2\left(
\frac1{24}D_y^3B
+\frac{\lambda}{4}D_y^2D_x
\right)+O(h^4).
\end{aligned}
\]

因此，在足够光滑的连续解上采样：
\[
\text{第二条离散方程的残差}=O(h^2).
\]

第一条则由连续双线性方程直接满足。

这一步已经说明：**它有正确的连续极限，并在双线性残差意义下二阶相容。** 但这还不是数值解的二阶收敛定理，也没有证明一般初值下的可解性。

**5. 不只检查展开：它确实有一个精确的一孤子形式**

设连续的一指数 tau 函数为
\[
f=1+A e^\theta,\qquad g=1+C e^\theta,
\qquad
\theta=kx+\omega t+\ell y+\theta_0,
\]
其中参数满足原双线性方程的代数关系。

对上述半离散候选，可以改为
\[
f_j=1+A E_j,\qquad g_j=1+C E_j,
\]
\[
E_j=R^j e^{kx+\omega t+\theta_0},
\]
其中
\[
\boxed{
R=\frac{1+h\ell/2}{1-h\ell/2}.
}
\]

这样得到的是候选半离散系统的**精确解**，不是近似代入。

它的含义是把连续的空间传播因子
\[
e^{\ell y}
\]
换成离散的
\[
R^j.
\]

而且，在合适的实数参数范围内，
\[
\frac{\log R}{h}
=\ell+\frac{h^2\ell^3}{12}+O(h^4),
\]
因此固定 \(y=jh\) 时，离散指数能够以二阶形式趋近连续指数。

一个具体例子是
\[
a=2,\quad \lambda=-2,\quad h=\frac1{10},
\]
\[
E_j=\left(\frac{77}{83}\right)^j e^{3x+3t+\theta_0},
\]
\[
\boxed{
f_j=1+\frac1{12}E_j,\qquad
g_j=1+\frac13E_j.
}
\]
它满足上面的两条半离散双线性方程；取实数 \(\theta_0,x,t\) 时，两个 tau 函数均为正。

**6. 为什么我暂时不把它称为“可积离散化”？**

我进一步测试了论文的双孤子形式：保留连续系统的相互作用系数，只把两个 \(y\) 指数分别换成上述离散传播因子。

选取
\[
a=2,\quad\lambda=-2,\quad h=\frac1{10},
\]
\[
(p_1,q_1)=(1,2),\qquad(p_2,q_2)=(0,3),
\]
代入后，第二条方程中 \(E_1E_2\) 项的系数为
\[
-\frac{27}{168490}\ne0.
\]

这证明了一个具体事实：**不能只替换指数传播因子，就把原来的双孤子解原样搬到这个候选系统。**

它不证明所有可积构造都不可能，也不排除其他解的形式；但说明“二阶相容＋一孤子通过”还不足以支撑可积性结论。若要追求与论文 mKP 结构相容的离散化，接下来需要从层级移位或 Bäcklund 关系重新约束离散方程，允许出现必要的修正项或辅助 tau 函数。

**7. 目前的适用条件与 Lean 验证范围**

这套候选的适用条件可以分开说：

| 目的 | 条件或限制 |
|---|---|
| 写出半离散双线性系统 | 均匀网格 \(h>0\)，保留所需的 \(x,t\) 导数 |
| 得到二阶残差估计 | 连续 tau 函数具有足够的 \(y\) 方向及混合导数，相关导数在考察区域有界 |
| 使用上述离散指数解 | \(1-h\ell/2\ne0\)；若要求实数正传播因子，可取 \(|h\ell|<2\) |
| 后续使用对数变量变换 | 需要排除相关 tau 函数的零点；实对数可先限制在正 tau 解族 |
| 求解初边值问题 | 还需指定边界、初值及约束；当前没有建立一般可解性和稳定性 |

我把以下四项代数核对加入了你的 Lean 项目，并已通过整个项目构建：

- 中点展开中的系数恒等式；
- 离散传播因子 \(R\) 的关系；
- 一指数解在边方程中的消去关系；
- 上述双孤子直接移植的非零残差。

证明文件是 [DLWSemidiscrete.lean](C:/Users/msz/学术内容/lean-toda/TodaFormalization/DLWSemidiscrete.lean)，另有[精确有理数检验程序](C:/Users/msz/学术内容/lean-toda/check_dlw_candidate.py)。

**Lean 当前证明的是有理数上的代数恒等式；Taylor 余项、一般解析解、稳定性和可积性不在这次形式证明范围内。**

这次已经完成一个可检查的半离散双线性基线。下一步最值得做的是利用双孤子检验暴露出的残差，结合原文的 mKP 层级结构改进构造；等结构确定后，再进行非线性变量变换和闭合。

############ ord 176  type=token_usage_record  2026-09-17T08:58:19.862Z ############

############ ord 177  type=event_msg  2026-09-17T08:58:19.870Z ############
